We will need the following simple lemma.

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
J\in\mathscr{S}(I)
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}(I)
, and let
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\geq 4
. Then the angle

\theta{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}

between
\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
and

\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
can be estimated as

\displaystyle|\theta{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}|\leq 3\,\theta_{n},\qquad\theta_{n}=\arctan\delta_{n}.

Proof.

The angle is trivially estimated by

\displaystyle\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\theta_{j}\leq\sum_{j=n}^{\infty}\delta_{j}\leq\frac{\delta_{n}}{1-1/r}=\delta_{n}\frac{2\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}-1}{\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}-1}\leq(7/3)\delta_{n}.

By the construction
\delta_{n}\leq q=0.1
, so using concavity of
\arctan
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
we get

\displaystyle\theta_{n}=\arctan\delta_{n}=\delta_{n}(\arctan\delta_{n})/\delta_{n}\geq\delta_{n}(\arctan 0.1)/0.1\geq 0.99\delta_{n}.

Thus the angle is bounded by
(7/2)\delta_{n}/0.99\leq 3\theta_{n}
,
which completes the estimate.
∎

Assume that
J\in\mathscr{S}(I)
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
, and that
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\geq 10
. We get from Lemma

5.4
that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}).

Since
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\perp\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
, Lemma
5.4
implies that
|(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\leq 3\theta_{n}\leq 3\delta_{n}
, so

\displaystyle\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{n}t_{k}\left[(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n})\right](\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{n}t_{k}\left[(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})\right].

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
J\in\mathscr{S}(I)
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
, and let
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\geq 10
.

(5.9)

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=-\alpha_{n}t_{k}\left[(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})\right]

(
with implied constants not depending on
n
,
k
,
I
,
J
,
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
)
.

Proof.

We have already shown above that
\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
gives
us
the desired form (the minus sign here is because of the minus in front of
t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
in (
5.6
)). As we claimed before,
that is
the main term, and we just need to show that the sum of the
other three inner products are estimated by
C\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n}^{2}
.

We have

|\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\leq\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\alpha_{n}\delta_{k}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\asymp}\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{k}^{2}\leq\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n}^{2};

here in the last inequality we used the fact that
\delta_{k}
is decreasing, and the equivalence
follows from the fact that

t_{k}\delta_{k}^{2}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\sim}\delta_{k}\alpha_{k}^{\#}\alpha_{k}\delta_{k}^{2}=\delta_{k}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k}^{\#}q^{2}\beta_{k}/\alpha_{k}=q^{2}\delta_{k}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\asymp}\delta_{k}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}.

On the other hand, trivially

|\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\leq\beta_{n}t_{k}=q^{-2}\alpha_{n}\delta_{n}^{2}t_{k}.

Finally
|s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}|\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}t_{k}
(because
\beta_{k}<\alpha_{k}
), so we
can
estimate

\left|\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\right|\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}\beta_{n}|s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}|\leq\beta_{n}t_{k}=\alpha_{n}\delta_{n}^{2}t_{k}.

Summing everything up, we get the desired estimate.
∎

We claim that the term
\alpha_{n}t_{k}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
is the main part of the term

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},

meaning that after summation (with
weights
|J|
)
it gives us the lower bound
\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\gtrsim}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3}
, while the rest gives a smaller
contribution
\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}
.

5.3.
Final estimates

Let us estimate the inner sum in (
5.1
).
Consider the sum
over the set
\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)=\mathscr{S}_{+}(I)\cap\mathscr{S}_{k}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|

\displaystyle=\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}-\alpha_{n}t_{k}\left[(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})\right]|J|.

Let us first estimate the main part

(5.10)

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}-\alpha_{n}t_{k}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|.

Note that only the factor

(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
depends on
J
, so the total sum
equals

\displaystyle-2^{-n-1-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},

where
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}
is the average of the vectors
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)
. There
are
2^{k-n-1}
intervals in
\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)
and the length of each interval
4^{-k}
,
which explains the factor
2^{-n-1-k}
.

To find the average
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}
let us recall how the vectors
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
,

J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)
are constructed. The set
\mathscr{S}_{+}^{n+1}(I)
consists of one interval
J=I_{++}
and
the vector
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
is the rotation of the vector
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
through the angle
\theta_{n}
.
The set
\mathscr{S}_{+}^{n+2}(I)
consists of the grandchildren
J_{++}
and
J_{-+}
of
J=I_{++}
, and
the
corresponding vectors are the rotations of
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
through the angles
\pm\theta_{n+1}

respectively, and so on.

Therefore, it is clear that the average
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}
must be a multiple of the vector

\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{++}}
. By Lemma
5.4
the angle between
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
and
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
cannot
be more than
3\theta_{n}<3\theta_{0}
, so for sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
the vectors
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
,

J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)
are almost parallel. Thus we can conclude that
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}=a_{k}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{++}}
, where
0.9\leq a_{k}\leq 1
.
By the construction

\displaystyle\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{++}}=\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}=-(\sin\theta_{n})\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}+(\cos\theta_{n})\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},

so

(5.11)

\displaystyle-(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=a_{k}\sin\theta_{n}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\geq}0.8\delta_{n}

(note that the signs worked out). Therefore, for all sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
, we can estimate

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}-\alpha_{n}t_{k}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|

(5.12)

\displaystyle\geq 0.8\cdot 2^{-n-1-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}\delta_{n}\geq 0.7\cdot 2^{-n-1-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n};

here in the last inequality we used the fact that
(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e})\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\geq}0.9(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}},\mathbf{e})=0.9
.

Remark 5.6
.

One can see from the above reasoning that for half of the intervals
J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)

there holds

-(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\geq-(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{++}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
.

If all inner products
(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
had the same sign (
\leq 0
) for all
J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)
,
one could use just this half of the intervals to get the estimate (
5.12
) (with a smaller
constant, which does not really matter).

Unfortunately, for
J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}
with sufficiently large
k
, not all inner products
(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
have the same sign. Namely, see (
4.14
) we can only
guarantee that

\theta_{j+1}\leq\theta_{j}/r
and we know that
1/r>1/2
. Moreover we know, see Remark

4.2
, that
\delta_{n+1}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\sim}\delta_{n}/r
, and since

\theta_{n}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\sim}\delta
, we conclude that there exists
q^{\prime}>0
,
1/r>q^{\prime}>1/2

such
that for all sufficiently small
\delta_{0}

\displaystyle\theta_{j+1}\geq q^{\prime}\theta_{j}.

Therefore, since
q^{\prime}>1/2
, we have for all sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j>n}\theta_{j}>\theta_{n}.

Recall that a
vector
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
is obtained from
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
by rotating it trough angle
\theta_{n}
, and then
through angles
\pm\theta_{j}
,
n<j<k
.
So, by picking sufficiently large
k
and all the rotations except the first one to be through

-\theta_{j}
,
j>n
, we get that for all sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
the corresponding vector

\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
is obtained from
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
by a
negative
rotation, so
-(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<0
.

Thus, a trivial estimate does not work, and we need a bit more involved reasoning; one possible
reasoning using the average of the vectors
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}

was presented above.

Continuing with estimating (
5.10
), let us estimate the non-essential parts.
Trivially

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}\alpha_{n}t_{k}O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})|J|\right|\leq C2^{-n-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n}^{2}

for all sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
.

Since
\delta_{n}\leq\delta_{0}
, we have that for all sufficiently small

\delta_{0}

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}\alpha_{n}t_{k}O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})|J|\right|\leq 0.2\cdot 2^{-n-1-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n},

and we can conclude that

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)}

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|\geq 0.5\cdot 2^{-n-1-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n}.

The sum over
\mathscr{S}_{-}^{k}(I)=\mathscr{S}_{-}(I)\cap\mathscr{S}_{k}
is estimated exactly the same. The difference is
that in this case the average
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}=a_{k}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-+}}
, and
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-+}}
is
the vector

\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
rotated through the angle
-\theta_{n}
, so

(5.13)

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=a_{k}\sin\theta_{n}\geq 0.7\delta_{n}

(for sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
), i.e. the inner product is positive, and not negative as
before. But we also have ‘‘
-
’’ in front of the sum, so we have the same estimate

\displaystyle-\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{-}^{k}(I)}

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|\geq 0.5\cdot 2^{-n-1-k}\alpha_{n}t_{k}\delta_{n}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\asymp}2^{-n-k}\alpha_{n}\widetilde{t}_{k}\delta_{n}.

Thus for the sum over
\mathscr{S}_{k}(I):=\mathscr{S}(I)\cap\mathscr{S}_{k}
we get

(5.14)

\displaystyle\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}(I)}

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\gtrsim}2^{-n-k}\alpha_{n}\widetilde{t}_{k}\delta_{n}.

Using (
4.11
) and formula (
5.7
) for
\widetilde{t}_{k}
, we see that
\widetilde{t}_{k}\geq r^{k-n}\widetilde{t}_{n}
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{k>n}2^{n-k}\widetilde{t}_{k}\geq\sum_{k>n}2^{n-k}\widetilde{t}_{n}\widetilde{r}^{n-k}=\frac{r/2}{1-r/2}\widetilde{t}_{n}=(2\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}-1)\widetilde{t}_{n},\geq 1.5\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\widetilde{t}_{n},

so we get from (
5.14
) that

\displaystyle 2\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{<}(I)}\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|

\displaystyle\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\gtrsim}2^{-2n}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\alpha_{n}\widetilde{t}_{n}\delta_{n}

\displaystyle\geq 2^{-2n}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\alpha_{n}q({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\alpha_{n}^{\#}\alpha_{n}})^{1/2}\delta_{n}

\displaystyle=2^{-2n}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}q^{2}\alpha_{n}({\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\beta_{n}^{\#}\alpha_{n}})^{1/2}

\displaystyle=2^{-2n}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}q^{2}\alpha_{n}.

Now, summing the above estimate over all
I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}
(i.e. over all
n\geq 1
), and recalling
that

\alpha_{n}\geq r^{n}\alpha_{0}
and that the set
\mathscr{S}_{n}
contains exactly
2^{n}
intervals, we get that

\displaystyle\|\Pi(W^{-1}\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}\mathbf{e})\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2}\geq{q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\alpha_{0}}\sum_{n\geq 1}(r/2)^{n}=q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}(2\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}-1)\alpha_{0}\geq q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3}\alpha_{0};

here we used the fact that
\sum_{n\geq 1}(r/2)^{n}=2\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}-1
. Recalling that

\displaystyle\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2}=\|W^{-1}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2}\asymp\alpha_{0}

completes the proof. ∎

6.
Lower bounds for Haar Shifts

In this section we prove the lower bound for the special Haar Shift
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
, which will be later
used to get the lower bound for the Hilbert Transform, i.e. to prove the main theorem (Theorem

1.1
).

This special operator
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
is defined as a linear combination of classical Haar Shifts, so let us
recall some definitions.
The classical Haar Shift
\mathord{\mathchoice{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}}

is defined by

\displaystyle\mathord{\mathchoice{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}}f=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{D}}(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}\left[h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}-h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}\right].

The importance of the Haar Shift comes from the fact that its average over dyadic grids yields a
non-zero multiple of the Hilbert transform.

In our construction we will use the
odd
Haar Shift
\mathbb{S}
, where the summation is taken over
the set

\mathscr{D}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm odd}}=\bigcup_{k}\mathscr{D}_{2k+1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}f=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{D}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm odd}}}(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}\left[h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}-h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}\right].

We will also need the
odd
modification
\mathbb{S}_{0}
of the operator
\mathord{\mathchoice{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}}_{0}
introduced in

[
10
]

\displaystyle\mathord{\mathchoice{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}}_{0}f

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{D}}\left[(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}-(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{0}f

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{D}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm odd}}}\left[(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}-(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}\right].

On vector-valued functions these operators are defined by the same formulas, if we, sightly abusing
notation, treat
(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}
as the vector

(6.1)

\displaystyle(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}:=|I|^{1/2}\langle fh{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}.

After the remodeling described below in Section
7
, the lower bound for the Hilbert
transform (for the remodeled weight) will be obtained
from the lower bound for the dyadic operator

\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}=c_{1}(\mathbb{S}-\mathbb{S}^{*})+c_{2}\mathbb{S}_{0};\qquad c_{1}\neq 0.

with
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
given by the identities (
7.9
) below.

We will use the lemma below with particular
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
given by (
7.9
), but it
remains true for any
c_{1}\neq 0
,
c_{2}
(with implied constants depending on
c_{1}
,
c_{2}
).

Lemma 6.1
.

For the matrix weight
W
we constructed in Section
4
and
f=W^{-1}\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}\mathbf{e}
there holds

\|\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\gtrsim}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}.

6.1.
Proving Lemma
6.1

In this section we always denote
f:=\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}
.

6.1.1.
Introducing sparse counterparts

We first notice that for each stopping interval
I\in\mathscr{S}
its
left sibling
L(I)
is a terminal interval, so the weight
W
is constant on children of
L(I)
,
and so is the function
f
. Therefore, in the representation
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f
a lot of terms are
0
, so we
can replace
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
in
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f
by its sparse counterpart
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}
.

Namely, for
I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}
let
\widehat{I}
be its dyadic parent. Then, defining sparse counterparts

\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}
,
\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}^{0}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}
of
\mathbb{S}
,
\mathbb{S}_{0}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
respectively by

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}f

\displaystyle:=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}\left[h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}_{+}}-h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}_{-}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}^{0}f

\displaystyle:=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\left[(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}_{+}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}_{-}}-(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}_{-}}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}_{+}}\right],

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}

\displaystyle:=c_{1}(\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}-\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}^{*})+c_{2}\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}^{0},

we can see that for
f=W^{-1}\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}\mathbf{e}
we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f

\displaystyle=\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}f.

The operator
\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}
can be represented as a linear combination of paraproducts

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}=2^{-1/2}\left(\Pi+\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{2}-\Pi_{3}\right)

where
\Pi
is the paraproduct introduced before in Section
5
, and

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}g=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\langle g\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L(I)}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L(I)},\qquad\Pi_{2}g=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\langle g\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L(I)}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\qquad\Pi_{3}g=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\langle g\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L(I)},

where, recall,
L(I)
is the left sibling of
I
.

6.1.2.
Essential part of
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f=\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}f

The following lemma shows that
2^{-1/2}(\Pi-\Pi^{*})f
is the main part of
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f=\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}f
,
and the rest can be ignored. That means the estimate

\displaystyle\|(\Pi-\Pi^{*})f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\gtrsim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}

would prove Lemma
6.1
.

Lemma 6.2
.

The norms of the operators
\Pi_{1}
,
\Pi_{2}
,
\Pi_{3}
,
\mathbb{S}^{0}_{\mathscr{S}}
and of their adjoints
are estimated above by
C\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
in
L^{2}(W)

(
and so in
L^{2}(W^{-1})
as
well
)
.

Remark 6.3
.

The above lemma implies immediately that for
f=\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}
and
sufficiently large
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
one has the lower bound

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{S}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\gtrsim}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}.

Indeed, for this particular
f

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}f=\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}f=2^{-1/2}\left(\Pi+\Pi_{1}-\Pi_{2}-\Pi_{3}\right)f,

and we have the desired lower bound for
\|\Pi f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}
. By Lemma
6.2
the
rest is small in comparison, and can be ignored.

To prove Lemma
6.2
we need some results from
[
31
]
.

Definition 6.4
.

Recall that a collection
\mathcal{C}
of dyadic intervals is called
\lambda
-Carleson
,

\lambda>0
, if for any
dyadic interval
J

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}(J)}|I|\leq\lambda|J|;

here we use the notation
\mathcal{C}(J):=\{I\in\mathcal{C}:I\subset J\}
.

Note that the collection
\mathscr{S}
of
stopping intervals is trivially
2
-Carleson, and therefore the collection
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}:=\{\widehat{I}:I\in\mathscr{S}\}
is
4
-Carleson.

We now restate Lemma 5.4 from
[
31
]
in the way adapted to our purposes. For a collection

\mathcal{C}
of dyadic intervals define the square function
S_{1}=S_{1,\mathcal{C},W}
as

(6.2)

\displaystyle S_{1}g(x):=\left(\sum_{I\in\mathcal{C}}\left\langle\|W(x)^{1/2}W^{-1/2}g\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\right\rangle_{I}^{2}\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}(x)\right)^{1/2}.

Here
g
is a function with values in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
W
is a
d\times d
matrix weight.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a
\lambda
-Carleson collection of dyadic intervals, and let
W\in\mathbf{A}_{2}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
,

[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}\leq\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
. Then for
S_{1}=S{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{1,\mathcal{C},W}
there holds

\displaystyle\|S_{1}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\leq C\lambda d\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}

with an absolute constant
C
.

It is essentially Lemma 5.4 from
[
31
]
applied to the weights
W^{-1}
and
W
for
W

and
V
in
[
31
]
. Lemma 5.4 from
[
31
]
was stated for the operator
\widetilde{S}_{1}

that dominates
S_{1}
.
The estimate there would translate to
Cd\lambda\left([W^{-1}]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}\right)^{1/2}\left([W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{\infty}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}\right)^{1/2}
, where

[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{\infty}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
is the dyadic
\mathbf{A}_{\infty}
characteristic of the weight
W
. We are not
giving the definition here, we only need to know that
[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{\infty}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}\leq 4[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
, see
[
31
, Remark 4.4]
.
Noticing that trivially

[W^{-1}]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}=[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
we get the estimate stated in Lemma

6.5
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

Trivially, for an operator
T
its norm in
L^{2}(W)
is the norm of the operator
g\mapsto W^{1/2}TW^{-1/2}g
(slightly abusing the notation we will say ‘‘of the operator
W^{1/2}TW^{-1/2}
’’) in the non-weighted space
L^{2}
. Since the intervals
L(I)
,

I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}
are disjoint,
we can estimate

\displaystyle\|W^{1/2}\Pi_{3}W^{-1/2}g\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\int_{L(I)}\|W(x)^{1/2}\langle W^{-1/2}g\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\int_{L(I)}\langle\|W(x)^{1/2}W^{-1/2}g\|\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\int_{\widehat{I}}\langle\|W(x)^{1/2}W^{-1/2}g\|\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\widehat{I}}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\left\|S{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{1,\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{<},W}g\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\|g\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{<}:=\{\widehat{I}:I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}\}
.
The crucial moment here is that the intervals
L(I)
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}
are disjoint, so we can
represent
the square of the norm as a sum of squares.

The estimate for
\Pi_{1}
works absolutely the same way: the only difference is that in the first
and the second lines one should take the averages over
L(I)
instead ot
I
. But the final
estimate will be the same.

As for
\Pi_{2}
, we can get the estimate by duality. Namely, replacing
W
by
W^{-1}
and repeating
for
\Pi_{2}^{*}
the same calculations as above with obvious
modifications we get that

\displaystyle\|W^{-1/2}\Pi_{2}^{*}W^{1/2}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|S{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{1,\widehat{\mathscr{S}}_{<},W^{-1}}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}.

Taking the adjoint we get that

\displaystyle\|W^{1/2}\Pi_{2}W^{-1/2}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}.

Finally, to estimate
\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}^{0}
we represent it as

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathscr{S}}^{0}=\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}-\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}^{*},

where
\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}
is the
sparse left shift
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}g=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}(g,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L(I)}.

Since the intervals
L(I)
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}
are disjoint, using the same estimates as above, we get
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)\to L^{2}(W)}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}.

Replacing
W
by
W^{-1}
we get that

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W^{-1})\to L^{2}(W^{-1})}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}},

which by duality implies that
\|\mathbb{S}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm L}}^{*}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)\to L^{2}(W)}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
.
∎

As it was discussed above, the lower bound for
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}_{\mathscr{S}}f
follows from the lower bound for

\|(\Pi-\Pi^{*})f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}.

We have the correct lower bound for
\|\Pi f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}
.
To see that
\|(\Pi-\Pi^{\star})f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}
also has the correct lower bound, it suffices
to show that for any sufficiently large
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}

\left(\Pi f,\Pi^{*}f\right)_{L^{2}(W)}\leq 0

for all sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
.

We calculate

(6.3)

\displaystyle-\left(\Pi f,\Pi^{*}f\right)_{L^{2}(W)}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\left(\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|I|

(6.4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{-}(I)}\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|

(6.5)

\displaystyle-\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}(I)}\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|

(6.6)

\displaystyle-\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}_{<}}\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}_{<}(I)}\left(\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|J|.

Here we expanded
\Pi f
indexing by
I
and
\Pi^{*}f
by
J
: the first term is the diagonal
term, the middle two terms correspond to the sum over
J\subsetneq I
and the last term corresponds
to
I\subsetneq J
but we swapped the roles of
I
and
J
. To address the middle two terms, we
compute for
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
(compare with (
5.5
))

(6.7)

\displaystyle\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}=\delta_{k}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}}{1+\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}+\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}\right);

it is essentially the same calculation as for (
5.5
), but with
\alpha_{k}^{\#}
,

\beta_{k}^{\#}
replaced by
\alpha_{k}

\beta_{k}
respectively, and the opposite sign because the
rotation is through
\theta_{k}
instead of
-\theta_{k}
in (
5.5
).
Therefore, for
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
,
k>n
,

\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}=s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}+t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J},

where

s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime}=\beta^{\#}_{k}\delta_{k}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}}{1+\delta^{2}_{k}}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime}=\alpha^{\#}_{k}\delta_{k}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}}{1+\delta^{2}_{k}}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=:t_{k}^{\prime}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

We also can easily see that

\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}=\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}+\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}

where

\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime}=\alpha_{n}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},\qquad\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime}=\beta_{n}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

We will need the following analogue of Lemma
5.5
.

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
J\in\mathscr{S}_{<}(I)
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
, and let
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\geq 10
.

(6.8)

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\alpha_{n}t_{k}^{\prime}\left[(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})\right]

(
with implied constants not depending on
n
,
k
,
I
,
J
,
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
)
.

The proof is exactly the same as the proof of Lemma
5.5
, so we skip it.

Let us use Lemma
6.6
to check the signs of lines (
6.4
),
(
6.5
). Let us fix
k>n
,
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
and let us average the right hand side of
(
6.6
) over all
J\in\mathscr{S}_{-}^{k}(I)=\mathscr{S}_{-}(I)\cap\mathscr{S}_{k}
. We will get

(6.9)

\displaystyle\alpha_{n}t_{k}^{\prime}\left[(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{2}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+O{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\delta_{0}<}(\delta_{n}^{2})\right],

where
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}
is the average of the vectors
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
over all
J\in\mathscr{S}_{-}^{k}(I)

(again with implied constants not depending on
n
,
k
,
I
,
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
). But we already computed the
average
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}
in Section
5.3
, and we know that

\displaystyle(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=a_{k}\sin\theta_{n}\geq 0.7\delta_{n},

see (
5.13
). Therefore the expression in brackets in (
6.9
) is bounded below
by
0.5\delta_{n}>0
(for
k>n
).
Summing up over all
n
,
k>n
, and recalling that
\alpha_{n}t_{k}^{\prime}\geq 0
we get that (
6.4
) is non-negative.

The treatment of the line (
6.5
) is exactly the same. Taking the average of the
right
hand side of
(
6.5
) over all
J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)=\mathscr{S}_{+}(I)\cap\mathscr{S}_{k}
, we again get
(
6.9
), but now
\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}
is the average of the vectors
\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
over
all
J\in\mathscr{S}_{+}^{k}(I)
.

But again, this average was already computed in Section
5.3
,
and we know that in this case

\displaystyle-(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\overline{\mathbf{b}}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{k}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=a_{k}\sin\theta_{n}\geq 0.7\delta_{n},

see (
5.11
). The ‘‘
-
’’ sign in the above inequality is compensated by the minus
in front of the sum in (
6.5
), so using the same reasoning as above we get that
the contribution of (
6.5
) is also positive.

Turning to the last sum (
6.6
), we first calculate using formula (
5.5
) for
\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}
that

\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}=s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}+t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J},

where (for
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
)

s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}=-\delta_{k}\beta_{k}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\#}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}^{\#}}{1+\delta_{k}^{2}}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\quad t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}=-\delta_{k}\alpha_{k}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\#}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}^{\#}}{1+\delta_{k}^{2}}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}=:t_{k}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

(
t_{k}
here is exactly
t_{k}
from (
5.7
)),
and

\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}=\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}+\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},

where

\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}=\delta_{n}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{n}-\widetilde{\beta}_{n}}{1+\delta_{n}^{2}}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\qquad\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}=\delta_{n}\frac{\widetilde{\alpha}_{n}-\widetilde{\beta}_{n}}{1+\delta_{n}^{2}}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

Therefore

\displaystyle\left(\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

(6.10)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}+\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}.

We observe that
(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0
and
(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0
and
recall that
(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0
and
(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{e}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}>0
. Then,
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}>0
and
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\#}-\widetilde{\beta}_{k}^{\#}>0

gives

\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<0,\qquad\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<0.

We roughly estimate the remaining two terms in (
6.10
), noticing that

\displaystyle 0\leq\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime},\qquad 0\leq-s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime\prime}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}-t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime\prime}.

Recalling that

|(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|,\,|(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\leq 3\delta_{n}\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}\delta_{0}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
, we then conclude that

\displaystyle|\sigma{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|,\,|\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}s{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\underset{\delta_{0}<}{\lesssim}-\delta_{0}\tau{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{\prime\prime}t{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{\prime\prime}(\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},

implying that for sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
the sum (
6.10
) is negative.
The minus sign in front of the sum (
6.6
) gives us a positive contribution there.

For the diagonal terms (
6.3
), let us figure out the sign of
\left(\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
.
The operators
\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
and
\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}
are given by
(
5.5
) and (
6.7
) respectively, so their product is a negative multiple
of the identity
\mathbf{I}
. Therefore the sign of
\left(\langle W\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}

is the same as the sign of

\displaystyle\left(\mathbf{e},\langle W^{-1}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<0,

which implies a positive contribution of the diagonal term.
∎

6.2.
The lower bound for the classical Haar Shift
\mathord{\mathchoice{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}}

Even though we will not need the lower estimate for the full Haar Shift for our main result, it is
of independent interest and it is not hard to complete the proof.

Theorem 6.7
.

For the weight
W
constructed above, and for the function
f=\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}

(
\mathbf{e}=\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}
, or
\mathbf{e}=\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}+\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}
)

\|\mathord{\mathchoice{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}{\mbox{Ш}}}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\geq c\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}.

Proof.

It was proved above, see Remark
6.3
, that for the odd Haar Shift
\mathbb{S}
and

f=\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}
we have the desired estimate
\|\mathbb{S}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\gtrsim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}
.
Therefore, to prove Theorem
6.7

it suffices to show that the contribution of the
even
Haar Shift
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
(where summation is
over the intervals in

\mathscr{D}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm even}}=(\mathscr{D}_{2k})_{k\geq 0}
),

\displaystyle\mathbb{S}^{\prime}f=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{D}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm even}}}(f,h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}\left[h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}-h{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}}\right],

is small in comparison.
Below we will show that for
the weight
W
and
our test function
f=\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{S}^{\prime}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)},

which will prove the theorem.

The weight
W^{-1}
, and so the test function
f=\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}
, are constant on children of
terminal intervals, see Section
4.5.2
, so the descendants of terminal intervals do
not contribute to
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}f
.
The terminal intervals do contribute to
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}f
, so expanding
\|\mathbb{S}^{\prime}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2}
we get

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{S}^{\prime}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2}

(6.12)

\displaystyle=\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}}\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}}\left(\left[\int W(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}})(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-}})\right]\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e},\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},

where, recall,

\mathcal{T}
is the collection of all
terminal intervals.

The estimate for the sum of the diagonal terms
I=J
can be easily reduced to Lemma

6.5
. Namely, we can estimate this sum as

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}}\left(\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}\mathbf{e}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I},\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}\mathbf{e}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|I|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}}\left\langle\|W(x)^{1/2}f\|\right\rangle_{I}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}}\left\langle\|W(x)^{1/2}W^{-1/2}W^{1/2}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\right\rangle_{I}^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x

\displaystyle=\|S_{1}W^{1/2}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}^{2},

where
S_{1}=S{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{1,\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T},W}
is the square function (
6.2
) with
\mathcal{C}=\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}
.

As we discussed above in Section
6.1.2
the collection
\mathscr{S}
is
2
-Carleson, therefore
the collection
\mathscr{S}\cup\mathcal{T}
is
4
-Carleson. Therefore, by Lemma
6.5

\displaystyle\|S_{1}W^{1/2}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\|W^{1/2}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}^{2}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2}.

To estimate the rest of the sum (
6.12
), let us thus consider one of the (symmetric) off
diagonal terms
with
J\subsetneq I
. If
I\in\mathcal{T}

then there is no contribution. If
J\in\mathcal{T}
then
\int W(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I_{-}})(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-}})=\pm\int W(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-}})=0
since
W
is constant on

J_{\pm}
, so again there is no contribution. Thus we only need to consider the case
I,J\in\mathscr{S}
.
Let us assume that
I\in\mathscr{S}_{n}
,
J\in\mathscr{S}_{k}
,
k>n
.

We calculate

\langle W(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-}})\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}=\frac{1}{4}\left[\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{++}}-\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+-}}-\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-+}}+\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{--}}\right].

Let us regroup the terms into differences corresponding to ‘‘neighboring’’ stopping and terminal
intervals. For the stopping intervals
J_{-+}
and
J_{++}
we get

(6.13)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left[\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{++}}-\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-+}}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\delta_{k}}{1+\delta_{k}^{2}}(\beta_{k+1}^{\#}-\alpha_{k+1}^{\#})(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{*}+\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{*});

we use the same calculation as for (
5.5
) with
\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\#}
,
\widetilde{\beta}_{k}^{\#}

replaced by
their ‘‘stretched’’ counterparts
\alpha_{k+1}^{\#}=r\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\#}
,
\beta_{k+1}^{\#}=\widetilde{\beta}_{k}^{\#}/(s_{k}r)
.

As for the terminal
intervals we get using the formulas (
4.6
) that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\left[\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+-}}-\langle W\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{--}}\right]=\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\delta_{k}}{1+\delta_{k}^{2}}\left(\left(2-\frac{1}{s_{k}r}\right)\widetilde{\beta}_{k}^{\#}-(2-r)\widetilde{\alpha}_{k}^{\#}\right)(\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}+\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}).

The exact coefficients are not essential here; what is essential is that in both cases we have
scalar multiples of the operator
\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}+\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}
, and the
absolute values of the coefficients are estimated as
\lesssim\delta_{k}\alpha_{k}^{\#}
.
2
2

2

Of course, to get to this conclusion we do not really need the tedious calculations
above: everything can be obtained just by simple analysis of the corresponding terms.

The same, of course, holds for the difference, so taking into account that
\|\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}+\mathbf{b}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\mathbf{a}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}^{*}\|=1
we get a trivial rough estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\langle W(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-}})\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\right\|\lesssim\delta_{k}\alpha_{k}^{\#}.

Recalling formula (
6.7
) for
\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}

we get

\displaystyle\left\|\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!J}\right\|\lesssim\delta_{k}\alpha_{k},\qquad\left\|\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\right\|\lesssim\delta_{n}\alpha_{n}.

So a rough estimate gives us

\displaystyle A{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}:=\left|\left(\langle W(\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{+}}-\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J_{-}})\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}\mathbf{e},\langle W^{-1}\widehat{h}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\rangle{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}\mathbf{e}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta_{k}^{2}\alpha_{k}\alpha_{k}^{\#}\delta_{n}\alpha_{n}

\displaystyle=q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\delta_{n}\alpha_{n}

\displaystyle=q^{3}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\beta_{n}^{1/2}\alpha_{n}^{1/2}

\displaystyle=q^{3}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}(\alpha_{n}/\alpha_{n}^{\#})^{1/2}.

Using (
4.15
) and identities
\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}=\alpha_{n}^{\#}\beta_{n}
,
\delta_{n}=q(\beta_{n}/\alpha_{n})^{1/2}
with
n=0
and the fact that
\alpha_{n}/\alpha^{\#}_{n}=\alpha_{0}/\alpha^{\#}_{0}
, see Remark

4.3
, we get

\displaystyle q\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{1/2}(\alpha_{n}/\alpha_{n}^{\#})^{1/2}=q\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{1/2}(\alpha_{0}/\alpha_{0}^{\#})^{1/2}=\delta_{0}\alpha_{0},

so

\displaystyle A{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}\lesssim q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\delta_{0}\alpha_{0}.

Multiplying by
|J|
and summing over all intervals we get

\displaystyle\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}}\sum_{J\in\mathscr{S},J\subsetneq I}A{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}|J|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{I\in\mathscr{S}}q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\delta_{0}\alpha_{0}|I|=2q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\delta_{0}\alpha_{0}\asymp q^{2}\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}\delta_{0}\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}^{2};

here we used the fact that for a stopping interval
I
the total length of the stopping intervals

J\subset I
of the next generation is
|I|/2
.

By picking sufficiently small
\delta_{0}
we can make the sum of off diagonal terms as small as we
want, so it can be ignored. Thus (
6.11
) holds, and Theorem
6.7
is proved.
∎

7.
Towards the lower bound for the Hilbert Transform: remodeling

Above we constructed a dyadic
\mathbf{A}_{2}
weight
W=W{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\!\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}}
on
I^{0}=[0,1)
,

[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
such that the dyadic operators, namely the paraproduct
\Pi
, the

odd
Haar Shift
\mathbb{S}
and the linear combination
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}=c_{1}(\mathbb{S}-\mathbb{S}^{*})+c_{2}\mathbb{S}_{0}
had lower
bounds
c\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}
.

In this section we will use the weight
W
to construct a weight
\widetilde{W}
such that
[\widetilde{W}]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\asymp[W]{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}=\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}
to prove later in Section
8

that the norm of the Hilbert
Transform
\mathcal{H}
in
L^{2}(\widetilde{W})
is bounded below by
c\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}
.

Since from Lemma
6.1
we have a lower bound for the operator
\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}
,

\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}=c_{1}(\mathbb{S}-\mathbb{S}^{*})+c_{2}\mathbb{S}_{0}
, there exist functions

f\in L^{2}(W)
,
g\in L^{2}(W)
,
\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}=\|g\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}=1
such that

(7.1)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f,g\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\gtrsim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}.

In our construction the functions
f
,
g
were the normalized in
L^{2}(W)

functions

\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}W^{-1}\mathbf{e}
and

\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f=\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}W^{-1}\mathbf{1}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I^{0}}\mathbf{e}
respectively, but in what follows we do not care what the
functions
f
and

g
are. Denoting
\mathbf{g}:=Wg
, and using
\mathbf{f}=f
for consistency sake, we can see that
\|\mathbf{f}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}=\|\mathbf{g}\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W^{-1})}=1
, and (
7.1
) can be rewritten as

(7.2)

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}\mathbf{f},\mathbf{g}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}}=\left(\mathcal{H}^{\scriptstyle\text{\rm dy}}f,g\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(W)}\gtrsim\boldsymbol{\mathcal{Q}}^{3/2}.

And using the construction presented below, we will show that (
7.2
) implies the lower
bound for the Hilbert Transform
\mathcal{H}
in
L^{2}(\widetilde{W})
for a weight
\widetilde{W}
remodeled from
W
.

Throughout this section we denote for intervals
I
,
J
by
\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}
the unique
orientation preserving affine bijection from
J
to
I
.

7.1.
(Iterated) periodization

Given a function
f
on an interval
I
define its
periodization

\mathcal{P}_{I}^{N}f
with
‘‘period’’
N
as the unique function on
I
with period
2^{-N}|I|
, consisting of repeated
2^{N}

‘‘shrunken copies’’ of the function
f
, i.e.
\mathcal{P}_{I}^{N}f=f\circ\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{N}
, where the
restriction of
\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{N}
onto each
J\in\operatorname{ch}^{N}(I)
coincides with
\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I,J}
. Note that

\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{N}
is a measure preserving transformation of the interval
I
.

Given a sequence
\vec{N}=(N_{1},N_{2},N_{3},\ldots)
of frequencies
N_{k}
and a function
f
on

I^{0}
let us define the
iterated periodization

\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}f
by induction as follows.

(i)

We start with the children
I^{0}_{\pm}
of
I^{0}
as the
starting intervals
of the first
order,
\operatorname{Start}_{1}
and define
f_{0}:=f
.

(ii)

Knowing the function
f_{k-1}
define the function
f_{k}
by replacing the function

f_{k-1}|_{I}

on every starting interval
I
of order
k
by its periodization
\mathcal{P}_{I}^{N_{k}}(f_{k-1}|_{I})
. We will call the descendants of order
N_{k}
of the starting intervals of order
k

the
stopping intervals
of order
k
.

(iii)

Define the starting intervals of order
k+1
as the grandchildren (
4
intervals in

\operatorname{ch}^{2}(I)
) of the stopping intervals of order
k
, and return to step (ii).

To avoid technical details with convergence we will only consider functions
f\in L^{1}(I^{0})
with
finite Haar expansion; in this case the process stabilizes after finitely many steps.

Since a periodization is obtained by composing a function with a measure preserving
transformation, the iterated periodization
\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}f
is also given by
\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}f=f\circ\Psi^{\vec{N}}
, where
\Psi^{\vec{N}}
is a measure preserving transformation.

7.1.1.
An alternative description of iterated periodization

Analyzing the process described above, we can see that the iterated periodization associates with a
starting interval
I
of order
k

a unique interval
J=\mathcal{F}(I)\in\mathscr{D}_{2k-1}
, such that
f_{k-1}|_{I}=f\circ\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J,I}
. Then, on

I
we perform the periodization of the
function
f\circ\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J,I}
. We will also associate the same interval
J=\mathcal{F}(I)\in\mathscr{D}_{2k-1}

with all
stopping subintervals
I^{\prime}\subset I
of order
k
, so
\mathcal{F}(I^{\prime})=\mathcal{F}(I)
for such intervals.

The function
\mathcal{F}
maps each starting (and stopping) interval
I
of order
k
to a
unique interval
\mathcal{F}(I)\in\mathscr{D}_{2k-1}
. Note that the function
\mathcal{F}
depends (only) on the
sequence
\vec{N}
of indices, so
\mathcal{F}=\mathcal{F}^{\vec{N}}
; however, to simplify our typography, we
will
often omit index
\vec{N}
.

Now let us see what happens when we apply the periodization to the martingale difference
decomposition of
f
,

(7.3)

\displaystyle f=\mathbf{E}_{1}f+\sum_{J\in\mathscr{D}_{\scriptstyle\text{\rm odd}}}\Delta{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{2}f.

Since the periodization does not change constant functions, we can see that
\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}\mathbf{E}_{1}f=\mathbf{E}_{1}f
, and the periodization
\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}\Delta{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{2}f
with
J\in\mathscr{D}_{2k-1}
depends
only on indices
N_{j}
,
j\leq k
. It is also easy to see that the starting intervals of order
k
,
and action of the function
\mathcal{F}
on these intervals depends only on indices
N_{j}
,
j<k
. Thus
for
J\in\mathscr{D}_{2k-1}
we can write

(7.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}\Delta{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{2}f=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\operatorname{Start}_{k}:\\
\mathcal{F}(I)=J\end{subarray}}\mathcal{P}{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{I}^{N_{k}}\left(\left(\Delta{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J}^{2}f\right)\circ\psi{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{J,I}\right).

Note that the position of the starting intervals
I\in\operatorname{Start}_{k}
,
\mathcal{F}(I)=J
depends only on
indices
N_{j}
,
j<k
.

Also, since the iterated periodization is given by a composition with a measure preserving
transformation, we can see that

(7.5)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}I\in\operatorname{Start}:\\
\mathcal{F}(I)=J\end{subarray}}|I|=|J|.

Figure 4.
The first intervals on which periodisation is applied are

\operatorname{Start}_{1}=\{I^{0}_{-},I^{0}_{+}\}
. The function
f|_{I^{0}_{-}}
is copied
2^{N_{1}}=8
times onto the
8
intervals in
\mathcal{D}_{4}\cap{I^{0}_{-}}
. The grandchildren
\mathcal{D}_{6}\cap{I^{0}_{-}}
of those
8
intervals constitute the new starting intervals
\operatorname{Start}_{2}\cap{I^{0}_{-}}
below
I^{0}_{-}
. Each of these grandchildren carries a copy of one of the grandchildren of
I^{0}_{-}
. For example the interval
J\in\mathcal{D_{3}}
marked in red is the second grandchild (counting from the left) of
I^{0}_{-}
. Correspondingly the intervals
I\in\operatorname{Start}_{2}\cap{I^{0}_{-}}
marked in red are the second grandchildren of intervals in
\mathcal{D}_{4}\cap{I^{0}_{-}}
. As such, the second difference
\Delta^{2}_{I}f
on those intervals is a compressed version of the second difference
\Delta^{2}_{J}f
through the map
\Psi_{J,I}
, with
J=\mathcal{F}(I)
.

Finally, the measure preserving property implies the preservation of products, norms, and inner
products:

Lemma 7.1
.

For
f,\,g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
with support on
I^{0}
we have

\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}(fg)=(\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}f)(\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}g),\quad\left(\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}f,\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}g\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\left(f,g\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})},\quad\|\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}=\|f\|{\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

7.2.
Remodeling

The iterated periodization will give us the desired lower bound for

\left(\mathcal{H}^{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}\mathbf{f},\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}\mathbf{g}\right){\vphantom{\rule[-2.79857pt]{0.0pt}{1.50696pt}}}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}
. Unfortunately,
while the weight
\mathcal{P}^{\vec{N}}W
has the same dyadic
\mathbf{A}_{2}
characteristic as
W
, its
\mathbf{A}_{2}

characteristic can blow up.

Fortunately, a simple idea, introduced
by F. Nazarov in
[
30
]
and further
developed later in
[
23
]
, allows one to pass from dyadic characteristics to the classical
ones.

7.2.1.
(Iterated) quasi-periodizations

We start with a dyadic
\mathbf{A}_{2}
weight, and we want to get a
regular
\mathbf{A}_{2}
weight (with comparable constant). Our weight
W
is generally not an
\mathbf{A}_{2}
